For
\Cref
eq:combine_magic_wand we give the proof:

\displaystyle~\left(\varphi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\left(\psi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}X\right)\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\left\{\inf\left\{X(s,h^{\prime\prime})\mid\psi(s,h_{\psi})=1,h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\psi}\right\}\mid\varphi(s,h_{\varphi})=1,h^{\prime}=h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\varphi}\right\}

(Definition of
\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\left\{X(s,h^{\prime\prime})\mid\psi(s,h_{\psi})=1,\varphi(s,h_{\varphi})=1,h^{\prime\prime}=h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\varphi}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\psi}\right\}

(
\Cref
eq:inf_partitioning)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\left\{X(s,h^{\prime\prime})\mid(\psi\mathbin{{\star}}\varphi)(s,h_{\psi}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\varphi})=1,h^{\prime\prime}=h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\varphi}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\psi}\right\}

(Definition of separation conjunction)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left(\left(\varphi\mathbin{{\star}}\psi\right)\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}X\right)(s,h)

(Definition of
\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}
)

For
\Cref
eq:sepimp_frame we give the proof:

\displaystyle~\varphi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}(X\mathbin{{\star}}Y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\{\sup\{X(s,h_{1})\cdot Y(s,h_{2})\mid h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\varphi}\}\mid\varphi(s,h_{\varphi})=1,h_{\varphi}\mathrel{\bot}h\}

(Definition of
\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}
and
\mathbin{{\star}}
)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\inf\{\sup\{X(s,h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\varphi})\cdot Y(s,h_{2})\mid h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h,h_{\varphi}\mathrel{\bot}h_{1}\}\mid\varphi(s,h_{\varphi})=1,h_{\varphi}\mathrel{\bot}h\}

(Decreasing the supremum)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\sup\{\inf\{X(s,h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\varphi})\cdot Y(s,h_{2})\mid\varphi(s,h_{\varphi}),h_{\varphi}\mathrel{\bot}h_{1}\}\mid h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\}

(
\Cref
eq:sup_inf_swap and decreasing the infimum)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup\{\inf\{X(s,h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\varphi})\mid\varphi(s,h_{\varphi}),h_{\varphi}\mathrel{\bot}h_{1}\}\cdot Y(s,h_{2})\mid h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\}

(Factorisation with constants)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left(\varphi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}X\right)\mathbin{{\star}}Y

(Definition of
\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}
and
\mathbin{{\star}}
)

∎

Definition 17
.

An expectation
X
is precise iff

\forall(s,h).~\left|\left\{h^{\prime}\in\textsf{{Heaps}}\mid\exists h^{\prime\prime}:h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h^{\prime\prime}=h~\text{and}~X(s,h^{\prime})>0\right\}\right|\leq 1~.

Lemma 18

(Properties for Precise Expectations)
.

For a precise expectation
X
and a precise qualitative expectation
\varphi
:

\displaystyle X\mathbin{{\star}}\min\left\{\,{Y},\>{Z}\,\right\}\quad=\quad\min\left\{\,{X\mathbin{{\star}}Y},\>{X\mathbin{{\star}}Z}\,\right\}

(Dist. with min)

(25)

\displaystyle X\mathbin{{\star}}(Y+Z)\quad=\quad(X\mathbin{{\star}}Y)+(X\mathbin{{\star}}Z)

(Dist. with plus)

(26)

\displaystyle\varphi\mathbin{{\star}}(Y\cdot Z)\quad=\quad(\varphi\mathbin{{\star}}Y)\cdot(\varphi\mathbin{{\star}}Z)

(Dist. with mult.)

(27)

Proof.

See
[
36
, Theorem 6.25]

∎

Appendix B
Proofs for the Weakest Safe Liberal Preexpectation

Lemma 19

(Monotonicity of
step
)
.

For all programs
C
and
t,t^{\prime}\colon C\to\mathbb{E}_{\leq 1}
we have

t(C)\leq t^{\prime}(C)\quad\text{implies}\quad\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\leq\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t^{\prime}\right)~.

Proof.

This follows directly by monotonicity of multiplication with probabilities, countable sums and infima.
∎

Lemma 20

(
0-1
Bounds on
wrlp
)
.

For all programs
C
, expectation
X
and qualitative expectation
\xi
we have

0\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\leq 1~.

Proof.

If
0\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\leq 1
, then the statement holds as well. We prove this new statement by induction on
n
.

For the base case
n=0
we have
0\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)=1
.

Now we assume an arbitrary but fixed
n
such that
0\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\leq 1
for all
C
,
X
and
\xi
.

For the induction step, if
C=\;\downarrow\;
the statement holds again trivially since expectations are also
0-1
bounded. Thus, we assume that
C\neq\;\downarrow\;
.
By the induction hypothesis
0\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\leq 1
, thus also
0\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\leq 1
for all
C^{\prime}
. If
t
is
0-1
bounded, then the
step
function is also
0-1
bounded:

\displaystyle\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h)=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot t(C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot 1\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(
\Cref
lem:monotone-of-step)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{1\mid a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\right\}

(MDP property)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h)=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot t(C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot 0\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(
\Cref
lem:monotone-of-step)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{0\mid a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\right\}

(0 is zero element)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

Lastly, the quantitative magic wand is the infimum of all applicable values for the second argument, thus also
0-1
bounded if their second argument is
0-1
bounded.
∎

Lemma 21

(Antitonicity of
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}
w.r.t.
n
)
.

For natural numbers
n\leq m
we have

\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\geq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{m}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)~.

Proof.

We instead prove by induction on
n
the equivalent statement that

\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\geq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)~.

For the base case
n=0
we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)=1
, thus the statement holds trivially by
\Cref
lem:zero-one-bounded.

Now for the induction hypothesis, we assume that for some fixed but arbitrary
n
we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\geq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)
for all
C
.

For the induction step, if
C=\;\downarrow\;
the statement holds trivially, since
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)=X=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+2}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)
. For
C\neq\;\downarrow\;
we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+2}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)=

\displaystyle\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)

(Monotonicity of
step
,
\mathbin{{\star}}
,
\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}
and induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)

∎

Lemma 22

(Alternative description of
wrlp
)
.

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)=\inf\left\{\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mid n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

Proof.

This follows from
\Cref
lem:antitone-of-wslp and real-valued analysis.
∎

Definition 23
.

Recall that

\displaystyle\text{reach}(n,\sigma_{1},\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})~=

\displaystyle~\sum\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}~~\mathbb{P}(\sigma_{i},\mathfrak{s}(\sigma_{1}\dots\sigma_{i}))(\sigma_{i+1})

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|\;\sigma_{1}\dots\sigma_{m}\in U^{m},\sigma_{m}=\sigma^{\prime},m\leq n\bigg]~.

For final states
\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\sigma~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{n}~\sigma^{\prime}\quad\text{iff}

\displaystyle\quad p~=~\text{reach}(n,\sigma,\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})~,

(28)

\displaystyle\sigma~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{1-p}^{n}~\dots\quad\text{iff}

\displaystyle\quad p~=~\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}~\text{final}}\text{reach}(n,\sigma,\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})~.

(29)

Lemma 24
.

For a scheduler
\mathfrak{s}
, a state
\sigma
and a final state
\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\sigma~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}\hskip-3.01125pt{}^{*}\hskip 3.01125pt~\sigma^{\prime}\quad\text{iff}

\displaystyle\quad p~=~\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}q_{n}~\text{with}~\sigma~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{q_{n}}^{n}~\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\sigma~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}\hskip-3.01125pt{}^{*}\hskip 3.01125pt~\dots\quad\text{iff}

\displaystyle\quad p~=~\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}q_{n}~\text{with}~\sigma~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{q_{n}}^{n}~\dots

Proof.

Both follow by using real-valued analysis.
∎

Lemma 25
.

For a scheduler
\mathfrak{s}
, a state
\sigma
and a final state
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
with
\sigma\neq\sigma^{\prime\prime}
and where
\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(\sigma\pi)
behaves as
\mathfrak{s}
with the difference that we always append
\sigma
to its argument, we have

\text{reach}(n+1,\sigma,\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sum\left[\;\mathbb{P}(\sigma,\mathfrak{s}(\sigma))(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\text{reach}(n,\sigma^{\prime},\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(\sigma\pi),\sigma^{\prime\prime})\mid\sigma^{\prime}\in U\;\right]

Proof.

\displaystyle\text{reach}(n+1,\sigma,\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathbb{P}(\pi_{i},\mathfrak{s}(\pi_{1}\dots\pi_{i}))(\pi_{i+1})\middle|\pi\in U^{m},\pi_{1}=\sigma,\pi_{m}=\sigma^{\prime\prime},m\leq n+1\right]

(by definition)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathbb{P}(\pi_{i},\mathfrak{s}(\pi_{1}\dots\pi_{i}))(\pi_{i+1})\middle|\pi\in U^{m},\pi_{1}=\sigma,\pi_{m}=\sigma^{\prime\prime},2\leq m\leq n+1\right]

(since
\sigma\neq\sigma^{\prime\prime}
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum\bigg[\mathbb{P}(\pi_{1},\mathfrak{s}(\pi_{1}))(\pi_{2})\cdot\prod_{i=2}^{m-1}\mathbb{P}(\pi_{i},\mathfrak{s}(\pi_{1}\pi_{2}\dots\pi_{i}))(\pi_{i+1})

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|\pi\in U^{m},\pi_{1}=\sigma,\pi_{m}=\sigma^{\prime\prime},2\leq m\leq n+1\bigg]

(by commutativity)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum\bigg[\mathbb{P}(\sigma,\mathfrak{s}(\sigma))(\pi_{1})\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathbb{P}(\pi_{i},\mathfrak{s}(\sigma\pi_{1}\dots\pi_{i}))(\pi_{i+1})

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|\pi\in U^{m},\pi_{m}=\sigma^{\prime\prime},1\leq m\leq n\bigg]

(renaming)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum\bigg[\mathbb{P}(\sigma,\mathfrak{s}(\sigma))(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\sum\big[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathbb{P}(\pi_{i},\mathfrak{s}(\sigma\pi_{1}\dots\pi_{i}))(\pi_{i+1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad~\big|\pi\in U^{m},\pi_{1}=\sigma^{\prime},\pi_{m}=\sigma^{\prime\prime},1\leq m\leq n\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|\sigma^{\prime}\in U\bigg]

(by distributivity)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum[\mathbb{P}(\sigma,\mathfrak{s}(\sigma))(\pi_{1})\cdot\text{reach}(n,\sigma,\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(\sigma\pi),\sigma^{\prime})\mid\sigma^{\prime}\in U]

(by definition)

∎

Definition 26

(Weakest Liberal Preexpectation after
n
steps)
.

\displaystyle\textsf{{wlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\right)\quad=

\displaystyle\quad\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]+p_{div}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}~\text{and}~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p_{div}}^{n}~\dots\bigg\}

Lemma 27
.

\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\right)\quad=\quad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\textsf{{wlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\right)

Proof.

Follows directly from
\Cref
lem:ntostar.
∎

Theorem 28

(Equality between
wlp
and
wlp
)
.

\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\quad=\quad\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\right)

Proof.

We first observe, that
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)
simplifies to

\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if}~n=0\\
X&\text{if}~n\neq 0,C=\;\downarrow\\
\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n-1}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)&\text{else.}\end{cases}

We now prove by induction on
n
that
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)=\textsf{{wlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\right)
, which by
\Cref
lem:wlpnandstar also proofs the claim.

For the induction basis, we have that
n=0
,
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s,h)=1
and that
\textsf{{wlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\right)=1
because of
\text{reach}(0,\sigma,\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})=0
and thus the probability of non-termination is
1
.

Now we assume that for some fixed but arbitrary
n
the claim holds.

For the induction step, we have two cases. If
C=\;\downarrow\;
, then
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s,h)=X(s,h)
; and
\text{reach}(n+1,(C,(s,h)),\mathfrak{s},(\;\downarrow\;,(s,h)))=1
, thus
\textsf{{wlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\right)(s,h)=X(s,h)
as well.

We will use lambda expressions to create anonymous functions. That is we define for a sequence of states
\pi
and a state
\sigma
the notation
(\lambda\pi^{\prime}.~\mathfrak{s}(\sigma.\pi^{\prime}))(\pi)=\mathfrak{s}(\sigma.\pi)
, where
\sigma.\pi
is the string concatenation of
\sigma
with
\pi
. We can split the first step apart from
\sigma~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}~\sigma^{\prime}
due to
\Cref
lem:smallstep. Thus, for
C\neq\downarrow
we have:

\displaystyle\textsf{{wlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{n+1}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]+p_{div}

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}~\text{and}~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p_{div}}^{n+1}~\dots\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{n+1}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\quad~+\sum\left[p_{div}\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p_{div}}^{n+1}~\dots\right]~\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\bigg\}

(
p_{div}
is unique)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p^{\prime\prime}\cdot p^{\prime}\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(C,(s,h).\pi)]{p^{\prime}}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\quad~+\sum\left[p_{div}^{\prime\prime}\cdot p_{div}^{\prime}\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p_{div}^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(C,(s,h).\pi)]{p_{div}^{\prime}}^{n}~\dots\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}~\text{and}~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(By
\Cref
lem:smallstep and since
C\neq\downarrow
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\bigg[p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\sum\left[p^{\prime}\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(C,(s,h).\pi)]{p^{\prime}}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\sum\left[p_{div}^{\prime}\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(C,(s,h).\pi)]{p_{div}^{\prime}}^{n}~\dots\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\bigg|~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\bigg]\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}~\text{and}~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Distributivity)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\inf\bigg\{\sum\bigg[p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\sum\left[p^{\prime}\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(C,(s,h).\pi)]{p^{\prime}}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\quad~+p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\sum\left[p_{div}^{\prime}\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(C,(s,h).\pi)]{p_{div}^{\prime}}^{n}~\dots\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigg|~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\bigg]\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\bigg\}\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(
\Cref
eq:inf_partitioning)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\bigg[\inf\bigg\{p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\sum\left[p^{\prime}\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(C,(s,h).\pi)]{p^{\prime}}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\quad+p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\sum\left[p_{div}^{\prime}\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}(C,(s,h).\pi)]{p_{div}^{\prime}}^{n}~\dots\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\bigg\}\bigg|~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\bigg]\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Expressiveness of Schedulers, see
\dagger
below)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\bigg[\inf\bigg\{p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\sum\left[p^{\prime}\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p^{\prime}}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\quad+p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\sum\left[p_{div}^{\prime}\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p_{div}^{\prime}}^{n}~\dots\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\bigg\}\bigg|~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\bigg]\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Set of schedulers where
C,(s,h)
has been taken and where it has not been taken is the same)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\bigg[p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p^{\prime}\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p^{\prime}}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad~~+\sum\left[p_{div}^{\prime}\mid C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p_{div}^{\prime}}^{n}~\dots\right]\bigg|~\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\bigg|~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\bigg]\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Infimum of multiplication with constants)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\bigg[p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\textsf{{wlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\right)(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\bigg|~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Rephrasing as
wlp
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\bigg\{\sum\bigg[p^{\prime\prime}\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})~\bigg|~C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p^{\prime\prime}}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)(s,h)

(Rephrasing as
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s,h)

(Rephrasing as
wrlp
)

The step in
\dagger
is the most difficult part here. We will not go into all details and instead give a proof sketch. The first direction (which is the same as in
\Cref
eq:inf_superlin), i.e.,

\inf\left\{\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in A}f(\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})\,\middle|\,\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\right\}\geq\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in A}\inf\left\{f(\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})\mid\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\right\}~,

it is easy (but technical) to proof: the value for choosing only one scheduler for all summands will always be at least as high as if we choose one scheduler each for every summand.
The other direction, i.e.,

\inf\left\{\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in A}f(\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})\middle|\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\right\}\leq\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in A}\inf\left\{f(\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})\mid\mathfrak{s}\in\mathbb{S}\right\}~,

is far more difficult to prove. Let
\sigma
be the current state and
\sigma^{\prime}
be the next state chosen in the sum. To prove this, one need to consider sequences of schedulers for each summand
\mathfrak{s}_{\sigma^{\prime},i}
such that they convert to the infimum, i.e.,

\inf\left\{f(\mathfrak{s},\sigma^{\prime})\mid\mathfrak{s}\in S\right\}=\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}f(\mathfrak{s}_{\sigma^{\prime},i},\sigma^{\prime})~.

From these, one can show that we can always construct a sequence of schedulers
\mathfrak{s}_{i}
for all summands such that
\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}_{i}(\sigma.\sigma^{\prime}.\pi)=\lambda\pi.~\mathfrak{s}_{\sigma^{\prime},i}(\sigma.\sigma^{\prime}.\pi)
. The expression then converges for the sequence
\mathfrak{s}_{i}
to the same value, i.e.,

\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in A}f(\mathfrak{s}_{i},\sigma^{\prime})=\sum_{\sigma^{\prime}\in A}\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}f(\mathfrak{s}_{\sigma^{\prime},i},\sigma^{\prime})~.

From both directions, the equality then also follows.

Thus, the claim is proven.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs about Framing

Definition 29
.

We define stacks
s
and
s^{\prime}
as equal for variables in
V
as

\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{V}\quad\text{iff}\quad\forall x\in V~s(x)=s^{\prime}(x)~.

Definition 30
.

We define the in program
C
written variables
\text{Write}(C)
as all variables occurring on left sides of assignments, lookups and allocation.

Lemma 31
.

For a
chpGCL
program without atomic region
C
and
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
we have
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
.

Proof.

We prove this by induction on
C
.

As the induction base we have that

•

for the terminated program, such a transition does not exist;

•

for the non-terminating program, probabilistic choice, conditional choice, loops, disposal and mutations, we always have
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s,h^{\prime})
, trivializing the statement; and

•

for assignment, allocation and lookup that the program on the left hand side is in
\text{Write}(C)
and thus does not change the value of variables in
\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)
.

Now we establish the induction hypothesis that for
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
and
C_{i},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C_{i},(s_{i}^{\prime},h_{i}^{\prime})
we have
\left(s\sim s_{i}^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C_{i})}
and prove the claim for the sequential composition and concurrency.

For the induction step we have that

•

for the sequential composition, we directly have that for
{C_{1}}{\,;}~{C_{2}},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~{C_{1}^{\prime}}{\,;}~{C_{2}},(s_{1}^{\prime},h_{i}^{\prime})
and
{\;\downarrow\;}{\,;}~{C_{2}},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C_{2}^{\prime},(s_{1}^{\prime},h_{i}^{\prime})
, the claim follows directly by the induction hypothesis;

•

for concurrency, we have for both cases of
i
in
\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[Ci.a]{p}~\left.C_{1}^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}^{\prime}\right.(s_{i}^{\prime},h_{i}^{\prime})
that the claim follows in both cases directly by the induction hypothesis.

∎

Lemma 32
.

For a
chpGCL
program
C
with atomic regions and
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
we have that
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
.

Proof.

We prove the claim with nested inductions. The first induction is on the level of atomic regions, i.e., we start with a program without any atomic region and increase the possible nested atomic regions in the induction step.
The second induction is on the number of steps on the operational semantics
n
.

Thus, we first start with a program that does not have any atomic region and prove that for
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
we have
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
. For
n=0
we only have
C=\;\downarrow\;
, thus trivially
s=s^{\prime}
. We establish the induction hypothesis
\dagger
for
n
and prove the claim for
n+1
. By
\Cref
lem:onestep_stackequal_wo_atomreg we have for
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h)
that
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
, furthermore by induction hypothesis
\dagger
we have for
C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p^{\prime}}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
that
\left(s^{\prime}\sim s^{\prime\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
. Together we also have by
\Cref
lem:smallstep that for
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}]{p^{\prime\prime}}^{n+1}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
we also have
\left(s\sim s^{\prime\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
.

Now we assume that for a fixed but arbitrary level of nested atomic regions the claim holds as our induction hypothesis
\spadesuit
.

For the induction step, we again prove by induction over n that
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
. We prove this again by induction over
n
. For
n=0
we have
C=\;\downarrow\;
, thus trivially
s=s^{\prime}
. We establish the induction hypothesis
\dagger\dagger
for
n
and prove the claim for
n+1
. The first part of the proof is for most cases totally analogous to
\Cref
lem:onestep_stackequal_wo_atomreg except for the atomic region. Thus, we only consider the case that
C=\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\}
Then we have the transition
\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[atomic]{p}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and by
\spadesuit
that for all
m
with
C^{\prime},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{m}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
also
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C^{\prime})}
, therefore also for
C^{\prime},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}\hskip-3.01125pt{}^{*}\hskip 3.01125pt~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
we have
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C^{\prime})}
by
\Cref
lem:ntostar and thus also for
\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[atomic]{p}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
we have
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
. For
\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[atomic]{p}~\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)
we trivially have
s=s
. With a similar proof as
\Cref
lem:onestep_stackequal_wo_atomreg, we get for
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
that
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
and from
\dagger\dagger
we get
C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}]{p}^{n}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
. Together and by
\Cref
lem:smallstep we get that for
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}]{p^{\prime\prime}}^{n+1}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})
we also have
\left(s\sim s^{\prime\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
.
∎

Lemma 33
.

For
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
we have
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)}
.

Proof.

Follows from
\Cref
lem:multiplestep_stackequal with
n=1
.
∎

Definition 34

(Free Variables)
.

We define the free variables of an expectation
X
as

\text{Vars}\,(X)\quad=\quad\{x\in\mathsf{Vars}\mid\exists v,v^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}:X\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]\neq X\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]\}~.

We define the free variables of a program
\text{Vars}\,(C)
as all variables occurring in the program
C
. We furthermore define
\text{Vars}\,(A_{1},A_{2},\dots)=\text{Vars}\,(A_{1})\cup\text{Vars}\,(A_{2})\cup\dots
.

Lemma 35
.

If
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{V}
and
\text{Vars}\,(X)\subseteq V
then
X(s,h)=X(s^{\prime},h)
.

Proof.

Assume there are stacks
s
and
s^{\prime}
with
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{V}
such that
X(s,h)\neq X(s^{\prime},h)
. Then
s
and
s^{\prime}
need to be different in at least one variable. For simplicity, we assume they differ in exactly one variable. Let
x
be this variable, such that
s(x)\neq s^{\prime}(x)
. Then
x\not\in V
since
\left(s\sim s^{\prime}\right)^{V}
and
x\not\in\text{Vars}\,(X)
since
\text{Vars}\,(X)\subseteq V
. Therefore, there exists no
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Vars}
such that
X\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]\neq X\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]
. Then there can also not exist
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Vars}
with
X(s\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h)\neq X(s\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right],h)
and therefore
s(x)=s^{\prime}(x)
, which violates our assumption. It is now easy to extend this proof to
s
and
s^{\prime}
which differ in arbitrary many variables using a sequence of stacks that only differ in one variable with their predecessor and successor each.
∎

Lemma 36

(Overapproximating Free Variables)
.

\text{Vars}\,(\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right))\subseteq\text{Vars}\,(C,X,\xi)

Proof.

We prove this by contraposition, i.e., that for every
x\in\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Vars}\,(C,X,\xi)
we have for all values
v,v^{\prime}
that
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]
. We will prove this by induction on
n
.

The induction base
n=0
holds trivially, since we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]=1=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]
.

We now assume that the claim holds for some arbitrary but fixed
n
as our induction hypothesis.

For the induction step, we have two cases. For the case that
C=\;\downarrow\;
we have the equality
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]=X\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]=X\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]
by assumption that
x\not\in\text{Vars}\,(X)
. For the case that
C\neq\;\downarrow\;
we have

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left(\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]

(Definition of
wrlp
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]

(
x\not\in\text{Vars}\,(\xi)
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\left(\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]\right)

(
\dagger
see below)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)

(
x\not\in\text{Vars}\,(\xi)
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)

(Induction Hypothesis)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\left(\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]\right)

(
x\not\in\text{Vars}\,(\xi)
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]

(
\dagger
see below)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left(\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]

(
x\not\in\text{Vars}\,(\xi)
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v^{\prime}}\right]

(Definition of
wrlp
)

Now regarding
\dagger
:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right](s,h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h)

(Definition of substitution)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C,(s\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime}\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h^{\prime})\mid C,(s\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime}\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h))\bigg\}

(Since
x\not\in\text{Vars}\,(C)
we have
s^{\prime}(x)=v
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime}\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right],h^{\prime})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Operational semantics and since
x\not\in\text{Vars}\,(C)
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot t(C^{\prime})\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right](s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of substitution)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\left[{x}\coloneqq{v}\right]\right)

(Definition of
wrlp
)

Thus, the claim is proven.
∎

Lemma 37
.

\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)=1
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
n
that
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)=1
. Then the same holds for the limit.

For the induction base
n=0
, we immediately have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)=1
.

Now we assume that the statement holds for some arbitrary but fixed
n
as our induction hypothesis.

For the induction step, we have:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\textsf{{step}}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)

(Definition of
wrlp
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}(\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi)

(
\dagger
, see below)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\xi\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}(1\mathbin{{\star}}\xi)

(Induction Hypothesis)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~1

(
\Cref
eq:reverse_modus_ponens)

Now we still miss
\dagger
, which we prove as:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum\left[1\cdot t(\textnormal{{diverge}})(s,h)\mid\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{div}]{1}~\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)\right]

(Operational semantics of divergence)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t(\textnormal{{diverge}})(s,h)

(Set is singleton)

Furthermore we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\leq 1
by
\Cref
lem:zero-one-bounded. Thus, the claim is proven.
∎

Lemma 38
.

For an atomic region free
chpGCL
program
C
, a mapping
t\colon C\to\mathbb{E}_{\leq 1}
and an expectation
X
with
\text{Vars}\,(X)\cap\text{Write}(C)=\emptyset
we have

\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X\quad\leq\quad\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)~.

Proof.

First, we notice that due to
\text{Vars}\,(X)\cap\text{Write}(C)=\emptyset
we have
\text{Vars}\,(X)\subseteq\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)
.
Now we prove the claim by structural induction on the program
C
.

For
C=\;\downarrow\;
we have
\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)=1
since
\text{Act}(\;\downarrow\;,(s,h))=\emptyset
, thus the inequality holds trivially due to
step
being bounded by
1
above (cf.
\Cref
lem:zero-one-bounded).

For
C=\textnormal{{diverge}}
we have
\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)=t(C)\mathbin{{\star}}X=\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X
(cf.
\Cref
lem:sounddiv)

For
C=x\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}e
we have

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot(t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h)\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum\left[(t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h)\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{assign}]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h)\right]

(
\text{Act}(C,(s,h))
is singleton)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\sum\left[(t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h_{1})\cdot X(s^{\prime},h_{2})\mid C,(s,h_{1})~\xrightarrow[\text{assign}]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h_{1})\right]

(for any
h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h
and every heap allows the assign action)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum\left[(t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\mid C,(s,h_{1})~\xrightarrow[\text{assign}]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h_{1})\right]

(by
\Cref
lem:onestep_stackequal,lem:expectation_stackequal and
\text{Vars}\,(X)\subseteq\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum\left[(t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h_{1})\mid C,(s,h_{1})~\xrightarrow[\text{assign}]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h_{1})\right]\cdot X(s,h_{2})

(Constant factors can be shifted outside)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})

Since the inequality holds for all
h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h
we also have

\displaystyle\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)~\geq

\displaystyle~\sup\{\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\mid h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s,h)~.

For
C=\left\{\,{C_{1}}\,\right\}\mathrel{\left[\,e_{p}\,\right]}\left\{\,{C_{2}}\,\right\}
the proof is analogous to the case
C=x\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}e
.

For
C=\textnormal{{if}}\,\left(\,{b}\,\right)\,\left\{\,{C_{1}}\,\right\}\,\textnormal{{else}}\,\left\{\,{C_{2}}\,\right\}
the proof is analogous to the case
C=x\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}e
.

For
C=\textnormal{{while}}\left(\,{b}\,\right)\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\}
the proof is analogous to the case
C=x\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}e
.

For
C={x}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\mathtt{new}\left(e_{0},\dots,e_{n}\right)

We now have that:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot(t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h)\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right\}

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot\sup\{t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{2})\mid h^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h^{\prime}_{2}=h^{\prime}\}\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
\mathbin{{\star}}
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[\sup\{t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{2})\mid h^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h^{\prime}_{2}=h^{\prime}\}\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{alloc-}\ell]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~\text{alloc-}\ell\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Operational semantics of allocation)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sup\Big\{\sum[t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{2})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{alloc-}\ell]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})]\Big|~h^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h^{\prime}_{2}=h^{\prime}\Big\}

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~\text{alloc-}\ell\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Sum over singleton or empty bag)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\inf\Big\{\sum[t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{2})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{alloc-}\ell]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})]

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~\text{alloc-}\ell\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\Big\}\bigg|~h^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h^{\prime}_{2}=h^{\prime}\bigg\}

(
\Cref
eq:sup_inf_swap)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\inf\Big\{\sum[t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s^{\prime},h_{2})\mid C,(s,h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2})~\xrightarrow[\text{alloc-}\ell]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2})]

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~\text{alloc-}\ell\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\Big\}\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

(Reducing bag for sum and set for supremum)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\inf\Big\{\sum[t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s^{\prime},h_{2})\mid C,(s,h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2})~\xrightarrow[\text{alloc-}\ell]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2})]

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~\text{alloc-}\ell\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h_{1}))\Big\}\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

(Since
\text{Act}(C,(s,h_{1}))\supseteq\text{Act}(C,(s,h)
))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\inf\Big\{\sum[t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\mid C,(s,h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2})~\xrightarrow[\text{alloc-}\ell]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2})]

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~\text{alloc-}\ell\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h_{1}))\Big\}\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

(
\Cref
lem:onestep_stackequal,lem:expectation_stackequal and
\text{Vars}\,(X)\subseteq\mathsf{Vars}\setminus\text{Write}(C)
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\inf\Big\{\sum[t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\mid C,(s,h_{1})~\xrightarrow[\text{alloc-}\ell]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})]

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~\text{alloc-}\ell\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h_{1}))\Big\}\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

(Definition of operational semantics)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\inf\Big\{\sum[t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\mid C,(s,h_{1})~\xrightarrow[\text{alloc-}\ell]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})]

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~\text{alloc-}\ell\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h_{1}))\Big\}\cdot X(s,h_{2})\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

(Constant factors can be shifted outside)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s,h)

(Definition of
\mathbin{{\star}}
)

For
C=\mathtt{free}(x)
we have

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

\mathbin{{\star}}

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h_{1}))\Big\}\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

(Constant factors can be shifted outside)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h_{1})\mathbin{{\star}}X(s,h_{2})\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s,h)

(Definition of
\mathbin{{\star}}
)

For
C=x\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,e\,\texttt{>}
we have

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h_{1}))\Big\}\bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\bigg\}

(Operational semantics of lookup)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup\bigg\{\inf\Big\{\sum[t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\mid C,(s,h_{1})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1})]

\mathbin{{\star}}

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

\displaystyle~(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s,h)

(Definition of
\mathbin{{\star}}
)

Now we assume as induction hypothesis that for arbitrary but fixed
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
the assumption already holds.

For
C={C_{1}}{\,;}~{C_{2}}
we have two cases, either
C_{1}=\;\downarrow\;
or
C_{1}\neq\;\downarrow\;
. We only consider the case that
C_{1}\neq\;\downarrow\;
, since the case
C_{1}=\;\downarrow\;
is analogous.

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot(t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\Big[p\cdot(t({C_{1}^{\prime}}{\,;}~{C_{2}})\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C_{1},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C_{1}^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\Big]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Operational semantics of sequential composition and assumption that
C_{1}\neq\;\downarrow\;
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t({C^{\prime}}{\,;}~{C_{2}})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t({C^{\prime}}{\,;}~{C_{2}})\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s,h)

(Induction Hypothesis)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s,h)

(Analogous to the previous transformation)

For
C=\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.
we have four cases, one for every case that we have for any
i=1
or
i=2
either
C_{i}\neq\;\downarrow\;
or
C_{i}=\;\downarrow\;
. We first prove it for the case that
C_{1}\neq\;\downarrow\;
and
C_{2}\neq\;\downarrow\;
. Other cases are analogous.

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot(t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\Bigg\{\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot(t(\left.C_{1}^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C_{1},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C_{1}^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C_{1},(s,h))\bigg\},

\displaystyle~\qquad\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[p\cdot(t(\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}^{\prime}\right.)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C_{2},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C_{2}^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C_{2},(s,h))\bigg\}\Bigg\}

(
\Cref
eq:inf_partitioning)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\Bigg\{\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(\left.C^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{1}\right.)\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h),\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C^{\prime}\right.)\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)(s,h)\Bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\inf\Bigg\{(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(\left.C^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{1}\right.)\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s,h),(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C^{\prime}\right.)\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s,h)\Bigg\}

(Induction Hypothesis)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\Bigg\{\sup\big\{(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(\left.C^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{1}\right.)\right)(s,h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\big|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\big\},

\displaystyle~\qquad~\sup\big\{(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C^{\prime}\right.)\right)(s,h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\big|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\big\}\Bigg\}

(Definition of
\mathbin{{\star}}
)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\sup\Bigg\{\inf\big\{(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(\left.C^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{1}\right.)\right)(s,h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2}),

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\quad\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C^{\prime}\right.)\right)(s,h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\big\}\Bigg|~h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\}\Bigg\}

(
\Cref
eq:sup_inf_swap)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sup\left\{\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h_{1})\cdot X(s,h_{2})\mid h_{1}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{2}=h\}\right\}

(Similar to the previos transformation)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\cdot X)(s,h)

(Definition of
\mathbin{{\star}}
)

Thus, the claim is proven.
∎

Lemma 39
.

For a
chpGCL
program
C
and expectations
X
and
Y
with
\text{Vars}\,(X)\cap\text{Write}(C)=\emptyset
we have

\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X\quad\leq\quad\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)~.

Proof.

We have
\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\right)=\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)
by
\Cref
thm:wlp-wslp-equality. Furthermore, if for all
n
we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mathbin{{\star}}Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)
we also have that
\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)
. We prove the claim now by a nested induction. The first induction is on the level of atomic regions.

We now assume that
C
does not have any atomic regions. The second induction is on
n
, For the base case
n=0
, we prove now first that
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X=1\mathbin{{\star}}X\leq 1=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)
. Next, we assume that the claim holds for a fixed but arbitrary
n
as our induction hypothesis
\dagger
. For our induction step, we have that either
C=\;\downarrow\;
in which case
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)=Y\mathbin{{\star}}X=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X
. For the other case that
C\neq\;\downarrow\;
we have:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)

(
\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]
is neutral element)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)

(Induction Hypothesis
\dagger
and monotonicity of
step
)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X

(
\Cref
lem:onestep_framing_wo_atomicregion)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X

(
\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]
is neutral element)

Next we assume that the claim holds for an arbitrary but fixed level of atomic regions in the program
C^{\prime}
as our induction hypothesis
\spadesuit
.

We again prove the induction step by an induction over
n
. For the induction basis we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X=1\mathbin{{\star}}X\leq 1=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)
. Thus, we assume that the claim holds for a fixed but arbitrary
n
as our induction hypothesis
\dagger\dagger
. For the induction step we have two cases. If
C=\;\downarrow\;
we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)=Y\mathbin{{\star}}X=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X
and if
C\neq\;\downarrow\;
we have:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)

(
\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]
is neutral element)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X

(Left to prove)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n+1}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X

(
\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]
is neutral element)

We require a similar proof as in
\Cref
lem:onestep_framing_wo_atomicregion together with our induction hypothesis
\dagger\dagger
to prove the left to prove part. There we only miss the case for atomic regions. Thus, we assume
C=\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\}
and prove first for
n=0
that:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)

(Case assumption)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\Big[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\Big]\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum\left[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

(Operational semantics of atomic regions)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\sum\left[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum\Big[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle~\qquad\quad\Big|~\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)\Big]

(Separating terminating and diverging pathes)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\sum\left[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum\Big[p\cdot 1\Big|~\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)\Big]

(
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}
is always
1
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\sum\left[p\cdot 1\mid\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum\Big[p\cdot 1\Big|~\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)\Big]

(
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}
is always
1
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\sum\left[p\cdot 1\mid C^{\prime},(s,h)~\xrightarrow{p}\hskip-3.01125pt{}^{*}\hskip 3.01125pt~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum\Big[p\cdot 1\Big|~C^{\prime},(s,h)~\xrightarrow{p}\hskip-3.01125pt{}^{*}\hskip 3.01125pt~\dots\Big\}

(Operational semantics of atomic regions)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(1\right)

(Definition of
wlp
)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(1\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)

(Monotonicitiy of
wlp
)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(1\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X

(
\spadesuit
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X

(Similar to above)

and next for
n>0
that:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\}\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)

(Case assumption)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\Big[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\Big|~\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{p}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\Big]\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h))\bigg\}

(Definition of
step
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum\left[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~C^{\prime\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

(Operational semantics of atomic regions)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\sum\left[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum\Big[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket\textnormal{{diverge}}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})

\displaystyle~\qquad\quad\Big|~\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)\Big]

(Separating terminating and diverging pathes)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\sum\left[p\cdot\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum\Big[p\cdot 1\Big|~\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)\Big]

(
\Cref
lem:sounddiv)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\sum\left[p\cdot(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum\Big[p\cdot 1\Big|~\textnormal{{atomic}}\left\{\,{C^{\prime}}\,\right\},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[\text{atomic}]{p}~\textnormal{{diverge}},(s,h)\Big]

(
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}
of
\;\downarrow\;
is always the postexpectation for
n>0
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\sum\left[p\cdot(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\mid C^{\prime},(s,h)~\xrightarrow{p}\hskip-3.01125pt{}^{*}\hskip 3.01125pt~\;\downarrow\;,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+\sum\Big[p\cdot 1\Big|~C^{\prime},(s,h)~\xrightarrow{p}\hskip-3.01125pt{}^{*}\hskip 3.01125pt~\dots\Big]

(Operational semantics of atomic regions)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)

(Definition of
wlp
)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X

(
\spadesuit
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X

(Similar to above)

Thus, the claim is proven.
∎

Theorem 40
.

For an
chpGCL
program
C
, a mapping
t\colon\textnormal{{{chpGCL}}}\to\mathbb{E}_{\leq 1}
and an expectation
X
with
\text{Vars}\,(X)\cap\text{Write}(C)=\emptyset
we have

\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)\mathbin{{\star}}X\quad\leq\quad\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~t(C^{\prime})\mathbin{{\star}}X\right)~.

Proof.

Follows from
\Cref
lem:onestep_framing_wo_atomicregion,lem:multiplestep_framing by structural induction on the program
C
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs for Conservativity Properties

Lemma 41

(Conservativeness of qualitative
wrlp
)
.

For qualitative
X
and non-probabilistic program
C
we have

\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)\in\{0,1\}~.

Proof.

The factor
p
in
step
is always
1
due to the absence of probabilistic constructs in
C
. The countable sum in
step
inflates to a sum of singleton or to the empty set. The sum of an empty set is
0
, the sum of a singleton is the value of its only element. From this point, the rest is a straight forward induction on
n
.
∎

Definition 42

(Framing Enabled Programs)
.

A non-probabilistic program
C
is framing enabled if for their respective
MDP
semantics we have for all states
C,(s,h)
, all heaps
h_{F}
with
h\mathrel{\bot}h_{F}
and all enabled actions
a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}))
that if there exists
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
then also
C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
.

Theorem 43
.

Every non-probabilistic
chpGCL
program
C
is framing enabled.

Proof.

We prove this by induction over the structure of
C
.

For the terminated program, non-terminating program, assignment, sequential composition, atomic region, conditional choice, loop, disposal, lookup and mutation we have
|\text{Act}(C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}))|\leq 1
.
If
\text{Act}(C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}))=\emptyset
the claim holds immediately.
Thus we assume that
\text{Act}(C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}))=\{a\}
. Now we furthermore assume that
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s,h^{\prime})
to show that the framing enabled condition holds. Then we also have for all these programs that
\text{Act}(C,(s,h))=\{a\}
.
Now let
\varphi(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})=1
iff
s^{\prime\prime}=s^{\prime}
,
h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}
and
\psi(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})=1
iff
h^{\prime\prime}=h_{F}
. If
\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\varphi\right)(s,h)=0
then the premise that
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s,h^{\prime})
is not satisfied. Thus we have that
\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\varphi\right)(s,h)=1
due to
\Cref
lem:01-qualitative-wslp. Then we also have that
1=(\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\varphi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\psi)(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})\leq\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\varphi\mathbin{{\star}}\psi\right)(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
due to
\Cref
thm:step_framing. Then

\displaystyle~\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\varphi\mathbin{{\star}}\psi\right)(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})=1

implies

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[(\varphi\mathbin{{\star}}\psi)(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\mid C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}))\bigg\}=1

(Definition of qualitative
step
)

implies

\displaystyle~\inf\bigg\{\sum\left[\varphi(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\cdot\psi(s^{\prime},h_{F})\mid C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})\right]

\displaystyle~\qquad\bigg|~a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}))\bigg\}=1

(Definition of qualitative
\mathbin{{\star}}
,
\varphi
and
\psi
)

which proves the claim, as the last equation can only be true if for (the only) action
a\in\text{Act}(C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}))
we have
C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
.

For allocation and for all
a\in\text{Act}({x}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\mathtt{new}\left(e_{1}\dots e_{n}\right),(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}))
, where we have that
{x}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\mathtt{new}\left(e_{1}\dots e_{n}\right),(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~\;\downarrow\;,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{a})
, we have since the action is enabled for
h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F}
also that
{x}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\mathtt{new}\left(e_{1}\dots e_{n}\right),(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~\;\downarrow\;,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{a}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
, which proves the claim.

Now we assume that the statement holds for arbitrary but fixed programs
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.

For sequential composition we have that if
C_{1}\neq\;\downarrow\;
and
{C_{1}}{\,;}~{C_{2}},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~{C_{1}^{\prime}}{\,;}~{C_{2}},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
then also
C_{1},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C_{1}^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
. By induction hypothesis
C_{1},(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C_{1}^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
and thus also
{C_{1}}{\,;}~{C_{2}},(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~{C_{1}^{\prime}}{\,;}~{C_{2}},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
. The case that
C_{1}=\;\downarrow\;
is analogous.

For concurrency we have that if
\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[C1,a]{1}~\left.C_{1}^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
, then we also have
C_{1},(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C_{1}^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
and by the induction hypothesis
C_{1},(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C_{1}^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
and thus also
\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[C1,a]{1}~\left.C_{1}^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
. The case where we have
\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[C2,a]{1}~\left.C_{1}^{\prime}\;\middle\|\;C_{2}\right.,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
is analogous and the case where we have
\left.\;\downarrow\;\;\middle\|\;\;\downarrow\;\right.,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[con-end]{1}~\;\downarrow\;,(s,h)
holds trivially.

Thus the claim is proven.
∎

See
4

See
2

Proof.

Remark that
step
simplifies for non-probabilistic programs to

\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h)=\inf\left\{0\,\middle|\,C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~\textnormal{{abort}}\right\}\cup\left\{t(C^{\prime})(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\,\middle|\,C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\right\}~.

(30)

We will first show that
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s,h)=1
satisfies all of these criteria for non-probabilistic and framing enabled programs:

1.

For
n=0
we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{0}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s,h)=1
, thus this criteria is satisfied.

2.

For
n>0
and
C=\;\downarrow\;
we have
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket\;\downarrow\;\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s,h)=\varphi(s,h)
, thus
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}
is
1
if and only if
\varphi(s,h)=1
.

3.

For
n>0
and for all
h_{\xi}
and
h_{F}
with
\xi(s,h_{\xi})=1
and
h\mathrel{\bot}h_{\xi}\mathrel{\bot}h_{F}
and that
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)(s,h)=1
then for all actions
a
we do not have
C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~\textnormal{{abort}}
, since else
\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(t\right)(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi})=0
by
\Cref
eq:simple-step and
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)(s,h)=0
, which contradicts our assumption. Finally since
C
is framing enabled we also get that we do not have
C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~\textnormal{{abort}}
as well.

4.

For
n>0
we have that if
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s,h)=1
, then for all
h_{\xi}
with
\xi(s,h_{\xi})=1
we have
\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n-1}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi})=1
by definition of
wrlp
and the magic wand. Furthermore, for all transitions
C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
we have
h^{\prime}=h^{\prime\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi}^{\prime}
with
\xi(s^{\prime},h_{\xi}^{\prime})=1
and
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n-1}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})=1
due to the separating multiplication with
\xi
. Finally since
C
is framing enabled, we also have this argument for
C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{F})
, finishing this direction.

Next we show that if all four criteria hold, then
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s,h)=1
:

1.

if
n=0
then
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s,h)=1
holds always.

2.

if
n>0
and
C=\;\downarrow\;
then
\varphi(s,h)=1=\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s,h)
.

3.

if
n>0
and
C\neq\;\downarrow\;
then we especially have for all
h_{\xi}
with
\xi(s,h_{\xi})=1
that the transition
C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~\textnormal{{abort}}
does not hold; but all three
C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi}^{\prime})
;
\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n-1}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})=1
; and
\xi(s^{\prime},h_{\xi}^{\prime})=1
does hold. Then we have:

\displaystyle\textsf{{step}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\lambda C^{\prime}.~\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n-1}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi\right)(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf\left\{0\,\middle|\,C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~\textnormal{{abort}}\right\}

\displaystyle\cup\left\{(\textsf{{wrlp}}_{n-1}\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket\left(\varphi\mid\xi\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi)(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi}^{\prime})\,\middle|\,C,(s,h\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi})~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~C^{\prime},(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\mathbin{{\star}}h_{\xi}^{\prime})\right\}

(
\Cref
eq:simple-step)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{0\,\middle|\,C,(s,h)~\xrightarrow[a]{1}~\textnormal{{abort}}\right\}
