Y\leq X\mathbin{{\star}}\left[{x\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]

disp

Y~\leq~\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket\mathtt{free}(x)\rrbracket\left(X\mid\xi\right)

The proof rules term, look, alloc, assign, mut and disp are sound by
\Cref
lem:soundbywlp and the proof rules for
wlp
from
[
5
]
.

X~\leq~\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\xi\right)

Y~\leq~\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(Z\mid\xi\right)

seq

X~\leq~\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket{C_{1}}{\,;}~{C_{2}}\rrbracket\left(Z\mid\xi\right)

The proof rule seq is sound by
\Cref
lem:soundseq.

X_{1}~\leq~\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\xi\right)

X_{2}~\leq~\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C_{2}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\xi\right)

if

The proof rule if is sound by

I\leq\left[{b}\right]\cdot X+\left[{\neg b}\right]\cdot Y

X_{1}~\leq~\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(Y\mid\xi\right)

X_{1}~\leq~\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C_{1}\rrbracket\left(Y_{1}\mid\xi\right)

\text{Write}(C_{i})\cap\text{Vars}\,(C_{3-i},Y_{3-i},\xi)=\emptyset

The proof rule concur is sound by

C
is AST wrt
S

Appendix F
Details on Examples

F.1
Additional Details on the Running Example

To recap, we are given the resource invariant
\xi=\max\left\{\,{\left[{r\mapsto 0}\right]},\>{\left[{r\mapsto-1}\right]}\,\right\}
and have already proven:

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{0.5\mathbin{{\star}}1~~|~~\xi}

\displaystyle\left.\begin{aligned} &\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{0.5~~|~~\xi}\\
&\left\{\,{\texttt{<}\,r\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}0}\,\right\}\mathrel{\left[\,0.5\,\right]}\left\{\,{\texttt{<}\,r\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}1}\,\right\}\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi}\end{aligned}\quad\middle\|\quad\begin{aligned} &\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi}\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1\mathbin{{\star}}\xi}\\
&y\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,r\,\texttt{>}\,;\,\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\max\left\{\,{\left[{y=0}\right]},\>{\left[{y=-1}\right]}\,\right\}\mathbin{{\star}}\xi}\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\max\left\{\,{\left[{y=0}\right]},\>{\left[{y=-1}\right]}\,\right\}~~|~~\xi}\\
&\textnormal{{while}}\left(\,{y=-1}\,\right)\left\{\,{y\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,r\,\texttt{>}}\,\right\}\,;\,\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{y=0}\right]~~|~~\xi}\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1\mathbin{{\star}}\left[{y=0}\right]~~|~~\xi}

The mutation
\texttt{<}\,r\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}-1
together with the atom rule gives us the inequality

0.5\mathbin{{\star}}\left[{r\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]\leq\left[{r\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}(\left[{r\mapsto-1}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}0.5\mathbin{{\star}}\max\left\{\,{\left[{r\mapsto 0}\right]},\>{\left[{r\mapsto-1}\right]}\,\right\})~.

However, it is easy to verify that
(\left[{r\mapsto-1}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}0.5\mathbin{{\star}}\max\left\{\,{\left[{r\mapsto 0}\right]},\>{\left[{r\mapsto-1}\right]}\,\right\})
simplifies to
0.5
, which results in the given lower bound.

For the probabilistic choice we have:

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{0.5~~|~~\xi}

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{0.5\cdot 1+0.5\cdot 0~~|~~\xi}

\displaystyle\left\{\,{\begin{aligned} &\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi}\\
&\texttt{<}\,r\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}0\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi}\end{aligned}}\,\right\}\mathrel{\left[\,0.5\,\right]}\left\{\,{\begin{aligned} &\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{0~~|~~\xi}\\
&\texttt{<}\,r\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}1\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi}\end{aligned}}\,\right\}

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi}

The right part is rather simple, as we can always lower bound anything by zero. The left part holds since with
\left[{r\mapsto 0}\right]
the mutation is satisfied, however the value before mutating
r
is unknown. We can lower bound the resulting preexpectation
1\mathbin{{\star}}\left[{r\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]
by
1\mathbin{{\star}}\max\left\{\,{\left[{r\mapsto-1}\right]},\>{\left[{r\mapsto 0}\right]}\,\right\}
and thus realise the resource invariant again.
For the loop invariant
\max\left\{\,{\left[{y=0}\right]},\>{\left[{y=-1}\right]}\,\right\}
we have:

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\max\left\{\,{\left[{y=0}\right]},\>{\left[{y=-1}\right]}\,\right\}~~|~~\xi}

\displaystyle y\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,r\,\texttt{>}

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\max\left\{\,{\left[{y=0}\right]},\>{\left[{y=-1}\right]}\,\right\}~~|~~\xi}

The lookup operation here results in both
\left[{y=0}\right]
and
\left[{y=1}\right]
to be evaluated to
1
if
\left[{r\mapsto 0}\right]
and
\left[{r\mapsto-1}\right]
, respectively. Our resource invariant guarantees this, thus we obtain the expectation
1
and lower bound it by
\max\left\{\,{\left[{y=0}\right]},\>{\left[{y=-1}\right]}\,\right\}
.
Lastly we check that it is indeed a loop invariant:

\displaystyle\left[{y=-1}\right]\cdot\max\left\{\,{\left[{y=0}\right]},\>{\left[{y=-1}\right]}\,\right\}+\left[{y\neq-1}\right]\cdot\left[{y=0}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[{y=-1}\right]+\left[{y=0}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\left\{\,{\left[{y=0}\right]},\>{\left[{y=-1}\right]}\,\right\}

F.2
Example: A Producer, a Consumer and a lossy Channel

Here we have the following program
C
:

\displaystyle l\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}0\,;\,

\displaystyle y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}k\,;\,

\displaystyle\left.\begin{aligned} &\textnormal{{while}}\left(\,{y_{1}\geq 0}\,\right)\left\{\right.\\
&\quad\left\{\,{x_{1}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}1}\,\right\}\mathrel{\left[\,0.5\,\right]}\left\{\,{x_{1}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}2}\,\right\}\,;\,\\
&\quad\texttt{<}\,z_{1}+y_{1}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}x_{1}\,;\,\\
&\quad y_{1}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}y_{1}-1\\
&\}\end{aligned}\quad\middle\|\quad\left.\begin{aligned} &\\[-10.00002pt]
&\textnormal{{while}}\left(\,{y_{2}\geq 0}\,\right)\left\{\right.\\
&\quad x_{2}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,z_{1}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\,;\,\\
&\quad\textnormal{{if}}\,\left(\,{x_{2}\neq 0}\,\right)\,\{\\
&\quad\quad\quad\{\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}x_{2}\}\\
&\quad\quad\mathrel{\left[\,p\,\right]}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\{\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}-1\}\,;\,\\
&\quad\quad y_{2}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}y_{2}-1\\
&\quad\}\\
&\}\\
&\\[-10.00002pt]
\end{aligned}\quad\middle\|\quad\begin{aligned} &\textnormal{{while}}\left(\,{y_{3}\geq 0}\,\right)\left\{\right.\\
&\quad x_{3}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\,;\,\\
&\quad\textnormal{{if}}\,\left(\,{x_{3}\neq 0}\,\right)\,\{\\
&\quad\quad\textnormal{{if}}\,\left(\,{x_{3}\neq-1}\,\right)\,\{l\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}l+1\}\,;\,\\
&\quad\quad y_{3}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}y_{3}-1\\
&\quad\}\\
&\}\end{aligned}\right.\right.

We use the resource invariant
\xi_{J}
for a set
J
. The set
J
encodes which locations in the array starting from
z_{2}
will have an error value of
-1
or a valid value of
1
or
2
after the channel inserts data into it. We use a big separating multiplication to connect all the possible instantiations using separating multiplication. That is,
\underset{}{\bigstar}\{X\}=X
and
\underset{}{\bigstar}(\{X\}\cup A)=X\mathbin{{\star}}\underset{}{\bigstar}A
for a non-empty and countable set
A
.
\xi_{J}
declares that all locations between
z_{1}
and
z_{1}+k
have either value
0
,
1
or
2
and all locations between
z_{2}
and
z_{2}+k
have values
0
,
1
or
2
if the offset is in
J
and
0
or
-1
if the offset is not in
J
. The value
0
is always possible for all locations between
z_{i}
and
z_{i}+k
since we assume
0
to be the initial value. We connect the predicates declaring possible values for the location
z_{j}+i
using a maximum, which acts as a qualitative disjunction here.

\displaystyle\xi_{J}\quad=

\displaystyle\quad\left(\underset{i\in\{0,\dots,k\}}{\bigstar}~~\max\left\{\,{\left[{z_{1}+i\mapsto 0}\right],\>\left[{z_{1}+i\mapsto 1}\right],\>\left[{z_{1}+i\mapsto 2}\right]}\,\right\}\right)

\displaystyle~\mathbin{{\star}}\left(\underset{i\in\{0,\dots,k\}\cap J}{\bigstar}\max\left\{\,{\left[{z_{2}+i\mapsto 0}\right],\>\left[{z_{2}+i\mapsto 1}\right],\>\left[{z_{2}+i\mapsto 2}\right]}\,\right\}\right)

\displaystyle~\mathbin{{\star}}\left(\underset{i\in\{0,\dots,k\}\setminus J}{\bigstar}\max\left\{\,{\left[{z_{2}+i\mapsto 0}\right],\>\left[{z_{2}+i\mapsto-1}\right]}\,\right\}\right)~.

We will leave out computations of inequalities
X\leq Y
for the sake of brevity and give an explanation instead. Since our representation of expectations may grow in size, we use a curly bracket after the
\fatslash~
symbol to denote expectations which are too long for one line. Our goal is to prove a lower bound on the probability that
l=|J|
is realised after termination. If
J
contains numbers outside the range between
0
and
k
, we may as well replace
|J|
with
|J\cap\{0,\dots,k\}|
. We now prove an invariant for each of the three subprograms.

For the producer
C_{1}
, we prove the invariant
I_{1}=1
with respect to the postexpectation
1
and resource invariant
\xi_{J}
. This shows indeed that the probability of safe execution of the loop is one and that our resource invariant
\xi_{J}
almost always holds.

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\left\{\,{\begin{aligned} &\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi_{J}}\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{1\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}\\
&x_{1}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}1\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{x_{1}\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}\end{aligned}}\,\right\}\mathrel{\left[\,0.5\,\right]}\left\{\,{\begin{aligned} &\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi_{J}}\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{2\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}\\
&x_{1}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}2\\
&\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{x_{1}\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}\end{aligned}}\,\right\}\,;\,

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{x_{1}\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\texttt{<}\,z_{1}+y_{1}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}x_{1}\,;\,

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle y_{1}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}y_{1}-1

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1~~|~~\xi_{J}}

The inequality

\left[{x_{1}\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}\leq\left[{z_{1}+y_{1}\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}(\left[{z_{1}+y_{1}\mapsto x_{1}}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}(1\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}))

resulting from the mutation
\texttt{<}\,z_{1}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}x_{1}
together with the atom rule holds because for
\left[{z_{1}+y_{1}\mapsto x_{1}}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}(1\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J})
to be non-zero,
x_{1}
must coincide with
\xi_{J}
– thus
x_{1}
has to be either
0
,
1
or
2
. Furthermore, we have that
\left[{z_{1}+y_{1}\mapsto i}\right]\leq\left[{z_{1}+y_{1}\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]
holds for every
i
, and obtain by this that
i\in\{0,\dots,2\}
, with which we re-establish
\xi_{J}
.
We have
\left[{y_{1}\geq 0}\right]\cdot 1+\left[{y_{1}<0}\right]\cdot 1=1
and therefore
1
is a loop invariant.

For the channel
C_{2}
, we use the shorthand notation
P(y)
to denote cumulated probability mass and define it as

\displaystyle P(y)\quad=\quad p^{|\{0,\dots,y\}\cap J|}\cdot(1-p)^{|\{0,\dots,y\}\setminus J|}~.

This shorthand notation gives us the probability that all data with offset
0
up to
y
are transferred according to
J
. That is, if an element should have been transferred successfully, we multiply with
p
and if not with
1-p
for every location up to
y_{2}
.
We prove for the invariant
I_{2}=\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]
with respect to the postexpectation
1
and resource invariant
\xi_{J}
:

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left[{x_{2}\neq 0}\right]\cdot\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})\\
&+\left[{x_{2}=0}\right]\cdot\left(\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]\right)\end{aligned}~~\mathchoice{\raisebox{-8.5pt}{\resizebox{327677}{1441792}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-8.5pt}{\resizebox{327677}{1441792}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-8.5pt}{\resizebox{301280}{1441792}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-8.5pt}{\resizebox{295820}{1441792}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle|$}}}}}~~\xi_{J}\right.

\displaystyle x_{2}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,z_{1}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\,;\,

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left[{x_{2}\neq 0}\right]\cdot\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)\cdot(p\cdot\left[{y_{2}\in J}\right]\cdot\left[{x_{2}\in\{1,\dots,2\}}\right]\\
&\quad\quad+(1-p)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\not\in J}\right])\\
&+\left[{x_{2}=0}\right]\cdot\left(\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]\right)\end{aligned}~~\mathchoice{\raisebox{-14.5pt}{\resizebox{327677}{2228224}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-14.5pt}{\resizebox{327677}{2228224}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-14.5pt}{\resizebox{301280}{2228224}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-14.5pt}{\resizebox{295820}{2228224}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle|$}}}}}~~\xi_{J}\right.

\displaystyle\textnormal{{if}}\,\left(\,{x_{2}\neq 0}\,\right)\,\{

\displaystyle\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)\cdot\left(p\cdot\left[{y_{2}\in J}\right]\cdot\left[{x_{2}\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]+(1-p)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\not\in J}\right]\right)~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\{

\displaystyle\quad\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left(\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)\right)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\in J}\right]\cdot\left[{x_{2}\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left(\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}=0}\right]\right)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\in J}\right]\cdot\left[{x_{2}\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}x_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}<1}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\left[\,p\,\right]}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\{

\displaystyle\quad\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left(\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)\right)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\not\in J}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left(\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}=0}\right]\right)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\not\in J}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}-1\,;\,

\displaystyle\quad\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}<1}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\}

\displaystyle\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}<1}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{2}-1\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}-1<0}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad y_{2}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}y_{2}-1

\displaystyle\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\}

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

We explain some of the difficult inequalities in the previous proof. We start with the inequality

\displaystyle~\left(\left(\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}=0}\right]\right)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\not\in J}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}(\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto-1}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\left(\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}<1}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}

resulting from the mutation
\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}-1
together with the atom rule. This inequality holds since for the part
\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto-1}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\dots
to be non-zero, we require
y_{2}\not\in J
due to
\xi_{J}
. We lower bound all evaluations where
y_{2}<0
by
0
as we can not infer any information about these locations from
\xi_{J}
. Afterwards, we can lower bound
\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]
by
\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto i}\right]
for every
i
and thus re-establish
\xi_{J}
.

Next we have the inequality

\displaystyle~\left(\left(\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}=0}\right]\right)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\in J}\right]\cdot\left[{x_{2}\in\{0,\dots,2\}}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}(\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto x_{2}}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}\left(\left[{1\leq y_{2}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)+\left[{y_{2}<1}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}

resulting from the mutation
\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}x_{2}
together with the atom rule. Here we assume that the location
y_{2}
is in
J
and obtain that
x_{2}\in\{0,\dots,2\}
. We lower bound any outcome of
y_{2}
not in
J
by
0
because we already know that we will eventually set the term to
0
due to the previous lookup. Next we establish
\xi_{J}
back from lower bounding
\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto\,{-}\,}\right]
.

We have the inequality

\displaystyle~\left[{x_{2}\neq 0}\right]\cdot\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{x_{2}=0}\right]\cdot\left(\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[{z_{1}+y_{2}\mapsto v}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}(\left[{z_{1}+y_{2}\mapsto v}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}

\displaystyle~(\left[{v\neq 0}\right]\cdot\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2}-1)\cdot(p\cdot\left[{y_{2}\in J}\right]\cdot\left[{v\in\{1,\dots,2\}}\right]+(1-p)\cdot\left[{y_{2}\not\in J}\right])

\displaystyle~+\left[{v=0}\right]\cdot\left(\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]\right))\mathbin{{\star}}\xi)

resulting from the lookup
x_{2}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,z_{1}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}
together with the atom rule. We will consider both cases separately. Let us assume that
v
is not
0
. Then either
y_{2}
is in
J
and
v
is either
1
or
2
to make
p\cdot\left[{y_{2}\in J}\right]\cdot\left[{v\in\{1,\dots,2\}}\right]
not zero, or
y_{2}
is not in
J
. Then, however,
v
needs to be
-1
, because else
\xi_{J}
will evaluate to zero. Both cases can then be used to turn
P(y_{2}-1)
into
P(y_{2})
. In both cases, we can also use
\left[{z_{1}+y_{2}\mapsto v}\right]
to re-establish the resource invariant
\xi_{J}
. If, on the other side,
v
is
0
, we do not get any new information, but also do not need to update
P(y_{2})
, and directly re-establish the resource invariant
\xi_{J}
.

Due to

\displaystyle~\left[{y_{2}\geq 0}\right]\cdot(\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right])+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]\cdot 1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right]

we establish the loop invariant with respect to postexpectation
1
.

For the consumer
C_{3}
we require a loop invariant that checks if
l
indeed matches the size of the set
J
. We prove the loop invariant
I_{3}=\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}+l=|J\cap\{0,\dots,y_{3}\}|}\right]
with respect to the postexpectation
\left[{l=|J|}\right]
and the resource invariant
\xi_{J}
:

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle x_{3}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{3}\,\texttt{>}\,;\,

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\left\{\begin{aligned} &\quad\left[{x_{3}=-1}\right]\cdot\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right]\\
&+\left[{x_{3}=-1}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]\\
&+\left[{x_{3}\neq 0}\right]\cdot\left[{x_{3}\neq-1}\right]\cdot\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l+1=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right])\\
&+\left[{x_{3}\neq 0}\right]\cdot\left[{x_{3}\neq-1}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l+1=|J|}\right]\\
&+\left[{x_{3}=0}\right]\cdot\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]\\
&+\left[{x_{3}=0}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]\end{aligned}~~\mathchoice{\raisebox{-32.5pt}{\resizebox{327677}{4587520}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-32.5pt}{\resizebox{327677}{4587520}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-32.5pt}{\resizebox{301280}{4587520}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-32.5pt}{\resizebox{295820}{4587520}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle|$}}}}}~~\xi_{J}\right.

\displaystyle\textnormal{{if}}\,\left(\,{x_{3}\neq 0}\,\right)\,\{

\displaystyle\quad\fatslash~~\left\{\begin{aligned} &\quad\left[{x_{3}=-1}\right]\cdot\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right]\\
&+\left[{x_{3}=-1}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]\\
&+\left[{x_{3}\neq-1}\right]\cdot\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l+1=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right]\\
&+\left[{x_{3}\neq-1}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l+1=|J|}\right]\end{aligned}~~\mathchoice{\raisebox{-20.5pt}{\resizebox{327677}{3014656}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-20.5pt}{\resizebox{327677}{3014656}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-20.5pt}{\resizebox{301280}{3014656}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-20.5pt}{\resizebox{295820}{3014656}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle|$}}}}}~~\xi_{J}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\textnormal{{if}}\,\left(\,{x_{3}\neq-1}\,\right)\,\{

\displaystyle\quad\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l+1=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l+1=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad l\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}l+1

\displaystyle\quad\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\}\,;\,

\displaystyle\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{3}-1\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}-1<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\quad y_{3}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}y_{3}-1

\displaystyle\quad\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\}

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

Here we will take a closer look at the inequality

\displaystyle~(\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}=0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{1,\dots,k\}|}\right])\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{v\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto v}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}(\left[{z_{2}+y_{2}\mapsto v}\right]\mathbin{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{$\boldsymbol{{-}\hskip-2.44444pt{-}}$}}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\raisebox{0.55974pt}{\rotatebox{-17.0}{$\star$}}}}

\displaystyle\quad(\left[{v=-1}\right]\cdot\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[{v=-1}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[{v\neq 0}\right]\cdot\left[{v\neq-1}\right]\cdot\left[{1\leq y_{3}\leq k+1}\right]\cdot\left[{l+1=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|}\right])

\displaystyle+\left[{v\neq 0}\right]\cdot\left[{v\neq-1}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}<1}\right]\cdot\left[{l+1=|J|}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[{v=0}\right]\cdot\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[{v=0}\right]\cdot\left[{y_{3}<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right])\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J})

due to the lookup
x_{3}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}\texttt{<}\,z_{2}+y_{2}\,\texttt{>}
together with the atom rule. We consider all cases separately.

•

First, let
v
be
-1

–

If moreover
y_{3}
is between
1
and
k+1
, then we can directly lower bound the case that
y_{3}
is
k+1
by zero as
\xi_{J}
does not have carry information for this location. Because
v
is
-1
, we know that
y_{3}
is not in
J
due to
\xi_{J}
. Thus, we also have that
|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|
.

–

If
y_{3}
is below
1
, the same reasoning holds, with the difference that we lower bound the expectation for every value of
y_{3}
below
0
as zero and consider only the case where
y_{3}
is
0
.

•

In the case that
v
is neither
0
nor
-1
, we first observe that only
1
and
2
are valid values, because
\xi_{J}
does not allow any other value for
y_{3}
between
0
and
k
.

•

In the cases where
v
is either
k+1
or below
0
, we just lower bound the formula by zero. However, for the latter cases we have
|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|+1=|J\cap\{y_{3},\dots,k\}|
.

•

Lastly, in the case that
v
is
0
, the expression already matches the target lower bound, but again, we lower bound the formula by zero if
y_{3}
has a value below
0
.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle~\left[{y_{3}\geq 0}\right]\cdot(\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k|\}}\right]+\left[{y_{3}<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]

and thus established a loop invariant with respect to postexpectation
\left[{l=|J|}\right]
.

Now we can combine all three results

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{P(k)\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq k}\right]\cdot P(k)+\left[{k<0}\right]\cdot\left[{0=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle l\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}0\,;\,

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{\left[{0\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=0}\right]\cdot P(k)+\left[{k<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\left\{\begin{aligned} &~1\\
\mathbin{{\star}}&~(\left[{0\leq k}\right]\cdot P(k)+\left[{k<0}\right])\\
\mathbin{{\star}}&~(\left[{0\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{k+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{k<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right])\end{aligned}~~\mathchoice{\raisebox{-13.97221pt}{\resizebox{327677}{2159046}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-13.97221pt}{\resizebox{327677}{2159046}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-13.97221pt}{\resizebox{301280}{2159046}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-13.97221pt}{\resizebox{295820}{2159046}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle|$}}}}}~~\xi_{J}\right.

\displaystyle y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\mathrel{\textnormal{{:=}}}k\,;\,

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\left\{\begin{aligned} &~1\\
\mathbin{{\star}}&~(\left[{0\leq y_{2}\leq k}\right]\cdot P(y_{2})+\left[{y_{2}<0}\right])\\
\mathbin{{\star}}&~(\left[{0\leq y_{3}\leq k}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J\cap\{y_{3}+1,\dots,k\}|}\right]+\left[{y_{3}<0}\right]\cdot\left[{l=|J|}\right])\end{aligned}~~\mathchoice{\raisebox{-13.97221pt}{\resizebox{327677}{2159046}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-13.97221pt}{\resizebox{327677}{2159046}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-13.97221pt}{\resizebox{301280}{2159046}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-13.97221pt}{\resizebox{295820}{2159046}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle|$}}}}}~~\xi_{J}\right.

\displaystyle\left.C_{1}\;\middle\|\;\left.C_{2}\;\middle\|\;C_{3}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\fatslash~~\vphantom{G^{\prime}}{1\mathbin{{\star}}1\mathbin{{\star}}\left[{l=|J|}\right]~~|~~\xi_{J}}

and we have for the whole program
C
and a set of schedulers
S\subseteq\mathbb{S}
:

\displaystyle~(P(k)\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right])\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\left[{l=|J|}\right]\mid\xi_{J}\right)

implies

\displaystyle~(P(k)\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right])\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}\leq\textsf{{wrlp}}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\left[{l=|J|}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}\mid\left[{\textsf{{{emp}}}}\right]\right)

(share)

implies

\displaystyle~(P(k)\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right])\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}\leq\textsf{{wlp}}^{S}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\left[{l=|J|}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}\right)

(
wlp
-
wrlp
)

We can use this to prove the lower bound of probabilities for even more elaborated postconditions if we have a set of schedulers
S\subseteq\mathbb{S}
such that
C
is almost surely terminating with respect to
S
. One of these is the probability that at least half of the messages are sent successfully, i.e., the probability of the postexpectation
\left[{k+1\geq l\geq\frac{k+1}{2}}\right]
– or equivalently
\sum_{\frac{k+1}{2}\leq j\leq k+1}\left[{l=j}\right]
. For this, we use the resource invariant
\xi_{j}=\max_{J\subseteq\{0,\dots,k\},|J|=j}\xi_{J}
where
\xi_{J}
is defined as previous. Although we call
\xi_{j}
a resource invariant, we never prove that it is a resource invariant. We only prove that
\xi_{J}
is a resource invariant. We can now compute:

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}^{S}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\left[{l=|J|}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}\right)\geq(P(k)\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right])\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}

implies

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}^{S}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\max_{J\subseteq\{0,\dots,k\},|J|=j}\left[{l=|J|}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}\right)\geq\max_{J\subseteq\{0,\dots,k\},|J|=j}(P(k)\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right])\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{J}

(max)

implies

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}^{S}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\left[{l=j}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{j}\right)\geq(p^{j}\cdot(1-p)^{k-j+1}\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right])\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{j}

(Definition of
\xi_{j}
)

implies

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}^{S}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\sum_{\frac{k+1}{2}\leq j\leq k+1}\left[{l=j}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{j}\right)\geq\sum_{\frac{k+1}{2}\leq j\leq k+1}(p^{j}\cdot(1-p)^{k-j+1}\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right])\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{j}

(Superlinearity)

implies

\displaystyle~\textsf{{wlp}}^{S}\llbracket C\rrbracket\left(\left[{k+1\geq l\geq\frac{k+1}{2}}\right]\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\left(\sum_{\frac{k+1}{2}\leq j\leq k+1}p^{j}\cdot(1-p)^{k-j+1}\cdot\left[{0\leq k}\right]\right)\mathbin{{\star}}\xi_{j}

(
\xi_{j}
is precise and
\left[{k+1\geq l\geq\frac{k+1}{2}}\right]
as above)

We could drop the resource invariant
\xi_{j}
inside
\textsf{{wlp}}^{S}
due to monotonicity of
\textsf{{wlp}}^{S}
, for which we do not provide a proof. However, this shows that we can use superlinearity to partition a big problem in smaller problems and afterwards reason about these smaller problems with the help of easier resource invariants, as it is standard in probability theory.
